## How to Use This Document

This folder (`ground-truth/gt-audit-20260430/`) contains two files that together form the complete audit package:

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **`proposed-changes.md`** _(this file)_ | Human-readable audit report with summary, per-benchmark change tables, and verifier instructions |
| **`audit_defects.json`** | Machine-readable structured data with full untruncated values for all 582 defects and 1,065 atomic field changes |

**The Markdown report is for review and orientation. The JSON file is the source of truth for the apply step** — it holds the full untruncated `proposed_fix.new_value` content that this report truncates to ~400 chars per cell for table readability.

## Instructions for Verifier

> **You are the INDEPENDENT VERIFIER.** Your role is to re-check every defect flagged by the 14 auditor agents and determine whether each proposed fix is correct, before any changes touch source files.

### Your Inputs

1. **`./audit_defects.json`** — the consolidated 582-defect file. Each entry has:
   - `test_id` — the GT row this applies to
   - `defect_id` — unique ID per defect
   - `defect_type` — one of `WRONG_CLAUSE_ATTRIBUTED` / `CONTENT_FABRICATED` / `MISSING_CLAUSE` / `CLAUSE_FORMAT_MISMATCH` / `OVER_PRECISE_CLAUSE` / `UNGROUNDED_CLAIM`
   - `field` — primary GT column allegedly defective
   - `current_value` — what's currently in the GT
   - `issue` — auditor's explanation
   - `evidence` — auditor's quoted PDF text and section references
   - `proposed_fix` — auditor's suggested correction with:
     - `field_to_change` — target GT column
     - `new_value` — proposed replacement (FULL untruncated text)
     - `additional_changes` — list of other field changes to make as part of the same fix
     - `rationale` — why this fix
   - `auditor_id` — which auditor flagged it

2. **`/tmp/clause_content_map.json`** — fast clause_id → text lookup (486 unique CCoP citations from indexed corpus).

3. **Authoritative regulatory PDFs** (priority order):
   - `ccop-official/CCoP---Second-Edition_Revision-One.pdf` (CCoP 2.0 — PRIMARY)
   - `ccop-official/RESPONSE-TO-FEEDBACK.pdf` (RTF)
   - `ccop-official/supplementary/Guidelines_for_Auditing_Critical_Information_Infrastructure.pdf`
   - `ccop-official/supplementary/Guide-to-Cyber-Threat-Modelling.pdf`
   - `ccop-official/supplementary/Guide-to-Conducting-Cybersecurity-Risk-Assessment-for-CII.pdf`
   - `ccop-official/supplementary/Security_By_Design_Framework.pdf`

### Your Three-Phase Task

**Phase 1 — Resolve the §11 conflict (do this first)**

Two auditors gave conflicting reports about CCoP 2.0 §11:
- Auditor E (B14/B18/B21) stated: "CCoP 2.0 ends at chapter 10"
- Auditor F (B22/B23/B24) stated: "§11 is DNSSEC (DOMAIN-SPECIFIC PRACTICES)"
- Earlier evidence (clause inventory) shows §11.2.2 exists in the corpus

**Read the CCoP 2.0 PDF table of contents and resolve definitively:** does §11 exist? What is its title? This affects ~80 defects in B22/B23 that hinge on whether §11 is the wrong place for waiver/coordination content.

**Phase 2 — Verify high-volume systemic claims (spot-check 5 rows each)**

Several auditors flagged whole-benchmark template defects. For each, sample 5 random rows from `audit_defects.json` and verify the foundational claim:

| Benchmark | Auditor's claim | Spot-check |
|---|---|---|
| B10 (39 defects) | All 20 rows cite §8.1 (Backup) | 5 random rows |
| B13 (43 ungrounded) | Fabricated "CIIO Audit Evidence Requirements" + "30-day pre-audit" in all 20 rows | 5 random rows |
| B14 (60 defects) | All 30 rows cite §6.1.1 (Logging) | 5 random rows |
| B18 (50 defects) | All 25 rows cite §8.1.1 (Backup) | 5 random rows |
| B22 (40 defects) | All 20 rows cite wrong §11 + §11(7) Act format mismatch | 5 random rows |

For each spot-check, either CONFIRM or REJECT the systemic claim.

**Phase 3 — Random-sample 30% of all 582 defects (~175)**

Pick ~175 defects at random across all benchmarks. For each:
- Read the auditor's `current_value`, `issue`, `evidence`, and `proposed_fix`
- Independently verify by looking up the cited clause in `/tmp/clause_content_map.json` AND/OR the PDF
- Verify the auditor's quoted clause text is accurate (auditors may have miscopied)
- Decide:
  - `APPROVED` — defect is real AND proposed fix is correct
  - `REJECTED_FALSE_POSITIVE` — auditor flagged a non-defect (no change needed)
  - `REJECTED_BAD_FIX` — defect real but proposed fix is wrong (return to auditor for new proposal)

**Phase 4 — Spot-check 20% of NO_DEFECT cases (~12 of 60)**

The original auditor reports include 60 NO_DEFECT entries. Sample 12 random ones. Verify yourself. If you find a missed defect, flag as `MISSED_BY_AUDITOR` with a suggested proposed fix.

### Your Outputs

1. **`/tmp/audit_verifier_decisions.json`** — JSON array of decisions, one per defect verified. Schema:
   ```json
   {
     "defect_id": "B06-001-D1",
     "test_id": "B06-001",
     "auditor_id": "auditor_B06",
     "decision": "APPROVED" | "REJECTED_FALSE_POSITIVE" | "REJECTED_BAD_FIX" | "MISSED_BY_AUDITOR",
     "decision_reason": "...",
     "verified_evidence": { "actual_clause_text_from_pdf": "...", "pdf_page": "..." },
     "proposed_fix_assessment": "..."
   }
   ```

2. **`/tmp/audit_verifier_summary.md`** — Markdown summary with:
   - §11 conflict resolution
   - Phase 2 systemic claim confirmations
   - Phase 3 sample stats: total checked, approved/rejected counts, per-auditor accuracy
   - Phase 4 missed-defect findings
   - Top 10 most concerning defects
   - Patterns of auditor accuracy/inaccuracy
   - Final recommendation: which defects safe to auto-apply, which need re-audit, which need human review

### Critical Constraints

- **Read-only.** Don't modify any source files.
- **PDF is authoritative.** When auditor's evidence quotes clause text, verify against PDF directly — auditors may have miscopied.
- **Be a strict critic.** Your job is to catch auditor mistakes. False positives become real GT modifications.
- **Use Python/Bash** for sampling and JSON handling. Don't construct large JSON inline in your messages.
- **Aim for ≤30 minutes total.** Sampling, not exhaustive re-audit.

2. **After verifier approval**, changes will be applied as follows:
   - Backup Excel: `CCoP_V2_Test_Cases_Expert_Review.xlsx.20260430-NNNNNN.bak`

3. **Post-fix verification** — re-run the 18-case hybrid eval against corrected GT and compare LLM Judge / RAGAS metrics against baseline.
